#### Public Question 1
- **Questioner Name:** Jim Ashenden of Floreat
- **Question Summary:** Question 1: Is the Town of Cambridge taking action to review the status of the old Skyline Drive land with a view to being involved in the provision of staged accommodation for ageing people?
- **Response Summary:** The subject land is owned by the Botanic Gardens & Parks Authority, is reserved under the Metropolitan Region Scheme and not identified for intensification by Council's adopted Local Planning Strategy.

#### Public Question 10
- **Questioner Name:** Graham Hornel, City Beach
- **Question Summary:** Question 3: Given the large expenditure on Beach Precinct Upgrades, why wasn't a specific Community Survey conducted in advance to determine actual and intended use of the Precinct by Cambridge Ratepayers and Residents?
- **Response Summary:** The lighting and CCTV project was initiated due to increased reports of petty crimes and feedback from WA Police. High mast light towers were replaced due to structural reports. Carpark resurfacing is driven by asset condition and the need to resurface.

**Source quote(s):**
> Although, for still unexplained reasons, this definitely did not happen towards securing Community Input on both the preferred date for our Town's Australia Day and Citizenship Ceremony and the now-approved extension of the e-scooter Trial, given the very large expenditure on the Beach Precinct Upgrades, why wasn't a specific Community Survey conducted in advance of Council project budget approval…
> The lighting and CCTV project was initiated due to increased reports of petty crimes such as car break ins during the summer months and feedback from WA Police. Further works such as the replacement of the high mast light towers was completed due to structural reports indicating the need to replace them due to age and condition. *(unverified)*

DONATES a cash amount of $20,000 to the Perron Institute, as described in limb 1(a) above. 3. > REQUESTS that Council directs the CEO: 1. To publish and maintain a publicly available register from 1 July 2024 of all development applications that include dwellings to be built into the primary 'front' setbacks (including primary and secondary for corner blocks) as outlined in the Section 26 of the Town's Local Planning Scheme. To provide a report to Council each month regarding development applications that recommend incursions into the primary 'front' setbacks (including primary and secondary for corner blocks) under the Local Planning Scheme with detailed explanation of the basis for the recommendation to allow incursions into the setback. 3. To report all incursions into the primary 'front' setback (including primary and secondary for corner blocks) to Council at least four (4) weeks BEFORE approval is provided.

#### M11 · 15.3.2 — Application to amend existing Development Approval - Ocean Village Precinct Redevelopment · *—*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—**

> Council to consider the application to amend the existing development approval for the 23-storey mixed-use redevelopment of the Ocean Village site, City Beach, and to determine a response to the Western Australian Planning Commission (WAPC) on the matter. The amended proposal seeks approval for reconfiguration of internal uses, minor design refinements, and enhanced landscaping including reduction in commercial floor space from 6,480 m² to 4,752 m², replacement of tavern and retail tenancies with 17 additional dwellings (total 264 dwellings), and improved landscaping and parking.

**Motion text:**
> That Council: 1. NOTES that the Western Australian Planning Commission has previously approved a 23-storey mixed-use development for the site under Part 17 of the Planning and Development Act 2005, and that the current amendment does not significantly alter the approved height or bulk of the development. 2. ADVISES the Western Australian Planning Commission (WAPC) that, within the context of the approved development, the Town: a) Supports the proposed design and parking improvements; b) Recommends that conditions be imposed requiring submission to the Town's satisfaction of a detailed Landscape Management Plan, Arboricultural Impact Assessment and Tree Protection/Method Statement, and that the following landscaping and public-realm requirements apply: i. New walls, paths, stairs and exercise stations are not supported within existing public open space or structural root zones (SRZs) due to impacts on tree protection areas and mature vegetation; ii. Any nature paths traversing tree zones must be supported by an arborist report and method statement demonstrating appropriate tree-retention measures; iii. Provide sufficient detail to assess rehabilitation areas, the Hale Road landscape buffer, and western path connections into Ocean Village Park; iv. Clarify the extent of tree removals associated with the Hale Road roundabout entry; v. Provide a full species schedule and planting plan; Corymbia calophylla (Marri) is not supported near verges or infrastructure—nominate suitable alternatives with arborist input; and c) Notes that all works and features within the public realm shown on the development plans are subject to separate approvals from the Town. d) Recommends that a revised Waste Management Plan (WMP) be submitted to the Town's satisfaction, which: i. Addresses the Town's three-bin FOGO collection system; ii. Applies WALGA waste-generation rates for residential and all commercial land uses; iii. Resizes the commercial bin store (Bin Area 2) to accommodate ~12 × 660 L bins (general waste, recycling and FOGO) based on WALGA rates and twice-weekly collection for the intended small-bar tenancy; and iv. Demonstrates compliant collection vehicle access and manoeuvring, bin staging, wash-down/containment, odour and noise management, and ensures bin presentation does not conflict with pedestrian/cyclist desire lines or SRZs; and e) Requests that all original subdivision and development conditions remain in place to secure civil, drainage and servicing requirements. f) Recommends that the WAPC ensure all design refinements and landscape commitments are implemented prior to occupancy. 3. AUTHORISES the CEO to provide, without prejudice, recommended conditions of development approval, for use in the event that the WAPC approves the amended development application.

**Planning:** Part 17C · Lot 800 Hale Road, City Beach · status: *PENDING*
